Ved kontroll av planleggingen bør utgangs punktet være selve konseptet og all nødven dig dokumentasjon - så som beregnings grunnlag, tegninger, materialspesifikasjo ner, bygge- og operasjonsprosedyre samt inspeksjonshåndbok. Beregningsunderlaget for konstruksjonens styrke bør, særlig for nye plattformtypers vedkommende, sees i lys av en total risikoanalyse eller vurdering. I størst mulig grad bør man tilstrebe uavhengighet i kontrollen. Kontrollmyndighetene bør såle des foreta uavhengige beregninger. Selv om en plattform er godkjent en gang er det av vesentlig betydning å foreta en reell, ny kontroll dersom det skulle være aktuelt å bygge denne plattformtypen noen år senere. Ny erkjennelse kan tilsi at endret syn på det aktuelle plattformsystemet. Godkjennelsen av nye plattformtyper bør fortrinnsvis skje i to faser nemlig på konsept og detalj stadiet. På denne måten blir det lettere å foreta eventuelle modifikasjoner som gjelder hovedsystemet. Ettersom siktepunktet med inspeksjonene under bygging og i driftsperioden bør være å oppdage grove feil, bør en gå lenger i retning av å foreta en tilstrekkelig undersøkelse av alle kritiske områder i stedet for å foreta en detaljert besiktelse av et lite område for å finne mindre feil slik som praksis til nå har vært. For at latente sprekkdannelser forårsaket av byggeprosessen skal være utløst, vil det være av vesentlig betydning at det utføres en omfattende kontroll av en ny plattform på lav dypgang i smult farvann første hele vinter etter at den er satt i kontinuerlig operasjon. For øvrig bør en hovedbesiktelse hvert annet år være tilstrekkelig. Kommisjonen forutset ter da at dens forslag fører til at skroginspek sjonen utføres med en betydelig større dek ningsgrad og grundighet enn det som hittil har vært vanlig. Det bør videre etableres et fastere opplegg for undervisning, trening og sertifisering av inspektører. Myndighetenes kontroll under bygging og operasjon er periodisk. En vil derfor i den mellomliggende tiden være av hengig av egenkontroll. Innholdet i egenkon trollopplegget før operasjonsfasen bør spesifi seres klarere og følges opp med motivasjon, opplæring o.s.v. Kommisjonens anbefalinger når det gjelder plattformers stabilitet skal omtales kort i det følgende. Stabilitetssvikt og konsekvensen av denne må generelt motvirkes ved å bygge inn sikkerhetsmargin mot kantring eller tilta gende fylling, og ved å ta i bruk strategier for å unngå at feil i planlegging og operasjon skal opptre, og for å oppdage og eliminere eventu elle feil og mangler som opptrer. Spesifikt innebærer dette først og fremst at lekkstabilitetskriteriene må revurderes. Så ledes bør skadeomfang eller fyllingsgrad som antas å initiere en skadet/lekk tilstand fast settes ut fra de erfaringer man har høstet og en analyse av mulige andre faresituasjoner. Det er ikke bare i tilfelle «Alexander L. Kielland» at en flytende plattform har tapt et hovedfly telegeme. Forskriftene bør også utformes slik at sik kerhetsmarginen for stabil flyteevne, spesielt i skadet tilstand, blir reell når en tar i betraktning de usikkerhetsmomenter som er til stede ved den formelle stabilitetskontrol len på planleggingsstadiet. Således taes det ikke hensyn til bølgenes og forankringssyste mets innvirkning. Vindmomentberegnin gene er særlig beheftet med usikkerhet. Des suten er beregningsmodellen for vannfylling forenklet. De forhold som hittil er nevnt, fører til at det bør stilles krav om reserve flyteevne. For enkelte plattformtyper vil et slikt krav kunne gjennomføres ved at det kreves reserveopp drift i dekket. Kravet bør dog formuleres som et funksjonskrav (dvs. krav for å unngå progressiv utvikling av ulykken) og ikke som et spesifikasjonskrav. I noen plattformer vil det nemlig være urimelig å kreve reserveoppside 205

unngå progressiv utvikling av ulykken) og ikke som et spesifikasjonskrav. I noen plattformer vil det nemlig være urimelig å kreve reserveoppside 205

drift i hele dekket, ettersom det finnes en reserve i den øvrige konstruksjon, samtidig som sikkerheten f.eks. med henblikk på å styrke kan være bedre enn i andre plattfor mer. Et funksjonskrav med hensyn på flyteev ne gir også større fleksibilitet ved den kon struktive utformingen til samlet å tilfredsstil le de delvis motstridende sikkerhetskrav som eksisterer. Hovedprinsippene for stabilitetskontrollen må forskriftfestes. Dessuten er det et behov for veiledning når det gjelder praktisk anven delse av forskriftenes bestemmelser. Mer de taljerte retningslinjer for stabilitetsberegnin ger, lekkstabilitetskriteriene, plassering av åpninger eig teknisk standard for luknings midler som gjør at de ikke blir å regne som fyllingsåpninger, samt operasjonsinstrukser bør inntas som ikke-bindende vedlegg til forskriftene. Gjennomføringen av kontroll av planleg gingen av stabilitetsrelevante forhold bør skje ut fra en helhetsvurdering. I planleggingsfasen bør uavhengig kontroll av stabilitetsberegninger - med EDB-baserte metoder som gir tilstrekkelig nøyaktighet - tilstrebes. Stabilitetsberegningen er meget sensitiv med hensyn på mulige fyllingsåpningers be liggenhet. Kontrollen av dette forhold bør tillegges stor vekt. Videre bør kontrollen med hva som skal godkjennes som realistiske operasjoner i nødsituasjoner skjerpes. Selv om ikke anormale vektsforhold synes å være medvirkende årsak til den foreliggende ulykken tilsier andre opplysninger kommi sjonen har innhentet en bedre oppfølging av vektsforholdene ombord - først ved utførelse av krengeprøve i forbindelse med bygging og siden kontinuerlig vektsregistrering under operasjon. Det bør også finnes en måte for oppfølging av at operasjonsinstrukser - særlig for nødsi tuasjoner - er kjent, forstått og kan anvendes av operasjonspersonell. Spørsmålet om reserve flyteevne må for øvrig sees i sammenheng med risikobildet totalt og andre risikoforebyggende tiltak, så som krav til reservestyrke 0.1. Forskriftenes krav til maksimal tillatt kren gevinkel må også bestemmes ut fra hensynet til funksjonsdyktigheten til maskineri, kon struksjon og forankringssystem, åpne evaku eringsveier og utsettingsmuligheter for red ningsutstyr, og ikke som nå, bare ut fra hensynet til tiltakende fylling og kantring. Lastelinjekonvensjonens anvendelse for flyttbare plattformer, og da først og fremst dens krav til lukningsmidlene over fribords dekk, bør klarlegges. 20 -NOU 1981: 11 Ved planlegging av plattformer må det legges stor vekt på å redusere muligheten for feil under operasjoner - særlig i nødsituasjo ner. Dette bør oppnås gjennom konstruktiv utforming blant annet ved hensiktsmessig plassering og innredning av kontrollrom, samt plassering av åpninger og valg av luk ningsanordning over disse. Dessuten bør det legges vekt på realistiske operasjonsprosedy rer, og mannskapets kjennskap, forståelse og anvendelse av disse. Motivasjon, opplæring og øvelser ved bruk av simulatorer for manøv rering, ballasting, forhaling o.lign. er en forut setning for dette. Konstruktiv utforming bør baseres på forutsetninger for dette. Kon struktiv utforming bør baseres på forutset ninger om utførelse av nødoperasjoner bare etter meget nøye vurdering. Når det gjelder beredskap, redningsutstyr m.v., tar kommisjonens anbefalinger sikte på å styrke beredskapen både på selve plattfor mene og beredskapen utenfor plattformene. Av anbefalinger som angår beredskapen utenom plattformene, kan nevnes: - Kommisjonen foreslår, eventuelt etter på legg fra myndighetene, et samarbeide mel lom rettighetshaverne i et område for å sikre den nødvendige beredskap. Gjennom en slik koordinering av rettighetshavernes beredskapsopplegg må man i en ulykkessi tuasj on gå ut fra at man vil få en hurtigere og mer effektiv assistanse fra de andre rettig hetshavere. - For alle plattformtyper - boreplattformer, boligplattformer og faste installasjoner - foreslår kommisjonen samme ordning med hensyn til hjelpefartøy dekning, og da den som i dag gjelder for boreplattformer. Det vil igjen si at det for hver plattform skal være ett hjelpefartøy stasjonert i en av stand av ikke mer enn 1 nautisk mil fra plattformen. - Forsyningsbåtene foreslår kommisjonen forsynt med redningsutstyr som setter dem i stand til på en effektiv måte å ta opp folk fra sjøen. - Kontinentalsokkelen foreslås inndelt i so ner, og i hver sone foreslås plassert red ningshelikopter med fastmontert red ningsheis og mannskap utdannet og trenet for redningsaksjoner. Et redningshelikop ter fra redningstjensten vil tilfredsstille disse krav. Helikoptere som fra base i land transpor terer personell til og fra plattformene, synes det i en ulykkessituasjon å være mulig å utnytte på en bedre måte enn hittil. Kom misjonen anbefaler at dette blir undersøkt nærmere. - Minst en gang hvert år foreslår kommisjo

være mulig å utnytte på en bedre måte enn hittil. Kom misjonen anbefaler at dette blir undersøkt nærmere. - Minst en gang hvert år foreslår kommisjo

side 206

nen at det skal avholdes en øvelse som omfatter de redningsmidler på feltet som inngår i beredskapsplanen, eventuelt den samordnede beredskapsplan for flere ret tighetshavere, rettighetshavernes bered skapsopplegg i land, ledelse og koordine ring av operasjonen, sambandslinjer m.v. Kommisjonens anbefalinger angående red ningsutstyr m.v. på selve plattformen gjelder dels kapasitetsutvidelse av et par av eksiste rende redningsmidler, dels forbedring av eks isterende redningsmidler og dels nye red ningsmidler. Kommisjonen foreslår for det første en livbåtdekning på 200 % for alle plattformty per. Forslaget er i samsvar med det krav som i dag stilles til faste installasjoner. Behovet for en overkapasitet av livbåter synes ikke å være mindre for boreplattformer og boligplattfor mer. For slike plattformtyper synes det realistisk å regne med at omtrent halvdelen av livbåtene vil bli satt ut av funksjon eller være utilgjengelige i tilfelle brann, krengning, ugunstige værforhold eller en kombinasjon av disse forhold. Kommisjonen foreslår videre at hver plattform skal ha en flåtedekning på 200 % for inntil 100 personer og 100 % dekning for overskytende antall personer. For bore plattformer vil forslaget i praksis ikke være til noen endring av flåtedekningen. For de andre to plattformtyper må flåtekapasiteten i noen grad økes. Men den livbåtdekning kommisjo nen har foreslått, anser kommisjonen det unødvendig å forlange at en del av flåtene skal kunne settes ut under godkjent utsettingsar rangement. Når det gjelder kommisjonens anbefalinger angående forbedringer av eksisterende red ningsutstyr, gjelder disse bare livbåtene. Det var i dette tilfellet livbåtenes utløsermekanis ke som sviktet. Systemet forbedres ikke ved å erstatte med livbåtkroker som kan utløses under belastning. Erfaringene fra en tidligere ulykke viser at man da risikerer at utløsning skjer i panikk i stor høyde fra sjøen. Kom misjonen anbefaler at man intensiverer arbeidet med å finne frem til en bedre og sikrere utløsermekanisme, eventuelt et helt annet utsettingsarrangement. Inntil så har skjedd, foreslår kommisjonen visse forbedrin ger av utløsermekanismen, som f. eks. for sterkning av beskyttelsesplater rundt utlø serkroken for å unngå at platene blir defor mert og at de dermed kommer til å sperre for utløserkroken. Videre foreslår kommisjonen diverse forbedringer med hensyn til livbåte nes utstyr og utrustning, så som mer klær og ulltepper om bord, refleks på overbygning og i bunn m.m. I tillegg til eksisterende rednings utstyr foreslår kommisjonen at personell kurv og redningsdrakter blir påbudt redning sutstyr. For så vidt angår redningsdrakter foreslår kommisjonen at hver mann om bord skal ha sin personlige redningsdrakt plassert i lugarene. Dessuten foreslår kommisjonen en ekstra redningsdraktdekning på 200 %. Disse drakter bør plasseres på hensiktsmessig måte i rømningsveiene og på livbåtstasjonene Kommisjonen mener at erfaringene fra denne ulykke viser at en så høy dekningsgrad av redningsdrakter er nødvendig dersom man mener at enhver som befinner seg om bord på en plattform, skal.ha en rimelig mulighet til å få tak i redningsdrakt i en situasjon som krever hurtig rømning av plattformen. I større oppholdsrom og korridorer bør inventar og tyngre gjenstander være festet. Gulvbelegget må gi feste for foten. Korridorer og rømningsveier bør videre være utstyrt med rekkverk. I tillegg til nødbelysningen bør korridorer og rømningsveier dessuten være utstyrt med separate, batteridrevne lamper som automatisk kobles inn dersom både hovedkraftkilder og nødkraftkilder svikter. Med hensyn til de kvalifikasjonskrav som bør stilles til dem som arbeider eller opphol der seg på en plattform, peker kommisjonen på at man ikke kan se noen grunn til at man bare skal ha forskriftsfestet krav om at det faste mannskap, herunder forpleiningsperso nellet, skal ha gjennomgått et nærmere defi nert sikkerhetskurs. Andre arbeidsstakere og de såkalte hotellgj ester har minst det samme behov for sikkerhetsopplæring som mann skapet. Fra kravet om sikkerhetsopplæring bør det bare dispenseres hvor det foreligger helt spesielle forhold. Opplæringen og de senere øvelser må om fatte bruk av absolutt alle de redningsmidler som vil være om bord i en plattform. Øvelsene bør utvides til også å omfatte prøving av rømningsveiene. Første gang en person kommer om bord på en plattform, bør han straks bli vist rundt på denne og under omvisningen uttrykkelig få påvist redningsut styrets plassering, den livbåtstasjon han til hører og rømningsveiene. Et klart siktepunkt med opplæringen og øvelsene må dessuten være å gjøre deltakerne sikkerhetsbevisste. Er man ikke motivert for sikkerhetsopplæ ring og øvelser, vil verdien av opplæringene og øvelsene bli svekket i vesentlig grad. Etter kommisjonens mening bør det for hver plattform utarbeides klare retningsli njer som skal godkjennes av offentlige myn digheter, for den instruksjon i arbeidet som på plattformen skal gis en ny mann før han påbegynner tjenesten om bord. Kommisjonens vurderinger og anbefalin ger er for alle punkters vedkommende en stemmige.

skal gis en ny mann før han påbegynner tjenesten om bord. Kommisjonens vurderinger og anbefalin ger er for alle punkters vedkommende en stemmige.

KAPITTEL 6 Summary 6.1 THE SETTING-UP OF THE INQUIRY COMMISSION AND ITS WORK The day after the loss of «Alexander L. Kielland», on the 28th March 1980, it was decided by the Norwegian Government through a Royal Decree that in accordance with the Norwegian Maritime Law, § 314, an investigation commision was to be set-up in connection with the accident. The Ministry of Justice and Police which was authorized to appoint the members of the Commission and to formulate the mandate, appointed the following members of the inquiry Commis sion: 1. Thor Næsheim, district judge, Sandnes, chairman 2. Torgeir Moan, professor at the Norwegian Institute of Technology, Division of Marine Structures, Trondheim 3. Per Bekkvik, platform manager/captain, Arendal 4. Aksel Kloster, secretary at the time of appointment, later local community enterprice manager, Stord 5. Sivert Øveraas, director, Norwegian Shipowners' Association, Oslo The mandate given to the Commission was to investigate the causes of the accident. Further the Commission was assigned to evaluate the functioning of the lifesaving appliances during the accident as well as how the evacuation and rescue operation took place and to propose recommendations in this connection. At an early stage the Commission engaged Kjell Straume, naval architect, as technical secretary. The Commission investigated the broken platform column - column D - as soon as this was towed to shore and also the lifeboats and other parts of lifesaving appliances as soon as these were found and brought on shore. Further the Commission had samples cut off the bracings connected to column D as well as the remaining part of bracing D-6 which was still on « Alexander L. Kielland» and ordered investigation and material testing at two laboratories of the parts cut off. The investigations which have been undertaken include, among other things, chemical, mechanical and metallographic investiga tions, together with examinations of the fracture surfaces. In addition the Commission asked different specialists to carry out hyd rodynamic calculations, strength calcula tions, stability calculations and an analysis of the anchoring system, to support its investigations. Also, experts assisted the Commission to register and survey the lifesaving appliances which were brought ashore. The Commission took testimony from all together 117 witnesses. Further the Commis sion has had at its disposal all the hearings as well as documents and other information of interest which has been brought up by the police hearings which have tåken place in parallel with the investigations undertaken by the Commission. Finally, the Commission has obtained a number of documents which concern «Alexander L. Kielland» and the accident, such as drawings of the rig, letters of approval and certificates, survey reports and so on. 6.2 BACKGROUND INFORMATION «Alexander L. Kielland» is a mobile plat form of the Pentagone type, which has been developed in France. Also the construction of the platform took place in France. The con tract between the French yard and the Norwe gian owners was signed at the end of 1973. The completed platform was delivered to the owners on the sth July 1976. «Alexander L. Kielland» was built as a drilling rig, but has during the entire period of its operation been utilized as an accommoda tion platform. It was first used with its original capacity of 80 beds. At four later occasions its accommodation capacity has been increased by mounting new accommodation containers on deck forward of the drilling tower. When the last accommodation containers were added in March/ April 1978, the accommoda tion capacity aounted to 348. «Alexander L. Kielland» was equipped with certificates as required by regulations issued by the Norwegian Maritime Directorate. The

aounted to 348. «Alexander L. Kielland» was equipped with certificates as required by regulations issued by the Norwegian Maritime Directorate. The

side 208

platform was classed with Det norske Veritas. The Maritime Diretorate as well as Det norske Veritas surveyed the rig according to their rules and regulations both during construc tion and operation. The inspections revealed only small and comparatively insignificant defects. The valid rules and regulations do not contain requirements regarding residual strength after a damage has occurred to the structure. However, damage stability requirements have been in existance and application. These requirements do not account for damages of the extent material ized on «Alexander L. Kielland» by the loss of the column D. Existing rules only require that the rig remains floating in a stable position after a damage which result in flooding of two adjacent tanks in a column. By the approval of «Alexander L. Kielland» watertightness was only required for the doors on top of the columns. This was due to the fact that the original stability calcula tions for «Alexander L. Kielland» indicated that the deck would not be submerged by damage to the structure. This again is directly connected to the fact that loss of "a column was not considered as an actual damage during the control of stability at the planning stage. The designer had, however, considered the water/weather tightness of the hull by giving the hull ample strength to withstand water pressure. This did, however, not lead to the consequence that the closing appliances were specified with the corresponding stan dard. The builders, however, have to large extent done so. The builders have worked out specification requirements regarding tigh tness of the closing appliances. The result was that especially the lower part of the deck was designed so that the platform could remain floating for a long time when closing applian ces were shut. In addition, the operational manual include requirements for tightening the closing appliances during operation ma nual all doors and ventilators on the columns are to be closed. The same applies to manhole covers, among others in oblique bracings, upper horizontal bracings and upper nodes. In addition, doors in athwartship bulkheads in deck as well as all outside hatches on main deck, are to be closed in bad weather. 6.3 THE LOSS AND ITS CAUSES On the 27th March 1980, the day of the accident, the «Alexander L. Kielland» lied at anchor on the Ekofisk field close to the production platform «Edda 2/7 C». This loca tion had nearly been the same in the last about 9 months. Due to the bad weather - bad visibility wind velocity of about 16-20 meter per second and wave heights about 6-8 meter - the platform was in the afternoon skifted somewhat away from «Edda 2/7 C». The gang way which connected the two platforms was hoisted onboard the «Alexander L. Kielland». About half an hour later, a few minutes before 1830 o'clock, column D broke off «Alexander L. Kielland». First bracing D-6 broke off. Then followed in rapid succession failures of the five other bracings which connected column D to the platform. These failures resulted from over loading. The fracture in bracing D-6 which initiated the structural failure, was a fatigue fracture. In this bracing there was cut out an opening for and welded in a support for an instrument for positioning control - called a hydrophone. The hydrophone support was fixed to the bracing by inside and outside fillet welds. Investigations undertaken after the accident show that the shape of the welding bead of the fillet welds was not the best. At the same time the penetration in the hydrophone support was too small. Low strength and ductility in direction of the plate thickness of the steel material and inferior quality of the fillet welds together with a high load level in the welds resulted in fractures in the welded connection between the hydrophone support and the bracing and also in the hydrophone support itself. Some of the fractures must have developed before the platform was mounted. This is proved, among others, by the fact that paint residues have been found on frac ture surfaces. These residues originate from painting which was carried out at the yard in Dunkerque. These fractures resulted in a stress redistribution. In this way two points in the bracing diamterically opposite to each other in the opening of the hyd rophone support, were subject to higher loading than before. Together with the rough fracture surfaces this again initiated fatigue cracks in the bracing itself at these two points. From both points the crack developed along the circumference of the bracing. In this way the bracing was weakened to such an extent that it no longer could withstand the loads to which it was exposed. When the final crack took place the cracks included about 2/3 of the circumference of the bracing. When the cracks had grown through the plate thickness of the bracing at most 12 months past before the final crack took place. It is possible, however, that this period of time has been substantially shorter. When column D was lost the «Alexander L. Kielland» at once heeled over until the angle of heel was about 30-35°.

time has been substantially shorter. When column D was lost the «Alexander L. Kielland» at once heeled over until the angle of heel was about 30-35°.

side 209

Then the platform stabilized in the sea. From this position it continued slowly to heel and to sink until it turned up-side down about 20 minutes later. The main reason for this unintended heel is that the platform was not designed to have sufficient stability when it loses a major buoyancy element (column). Neither did any regulation require stability for such circumst ances at the time when «Alexander L. Kiel land» was approved. When «Alexander L. Kielland» heeled over downflooding of water took place at once in the trunks, which include the shafts of the lifts, in column E and column C and possibly also in oblique bracings and the dry tank at the top of column E. Filling of the trunks and possibly also the oblique bracings was not sufficient to make «Alexander L. Kielland» capsize. In order that that should happen also more than half of the deck volume would have to be filled up with water. On the condition that filling of the deck volume to such a degree should occur only by flooding through open dram valves in the lower deck, the capsizing would have tåken place more than an hour after the initial heeling. When «Alexander L. Kielland» turned up-side down just after about 20 minutes flooding of the deck volume must have tåken place also through other openings in the deck - doors or ventilators. However, the possibility that hoies caused by damage have been downflooding openings can not be completely ruled out. The main reason for the structural failure is an unfortunate design, dimensioning and material quality of the hydrophone support as well as its connection to the bracing. The reason for this is that the hydrophone support was considered as outfit and not as a part of the load carrying construction at the plan ning stage. In addition to the unfortunate design the control of the planning of design as well as the surveys accomplished during building and operation were obviously deficient in order to unveil crack defects. The reason for this is both that the intentions of the regulations of annuai inspections as laid down in rules and regulations do not seem to have been fulfilled by the inspections which were undertaken, and also - and to a larger extent - that the surveys required by rules and regulations even by an ideal practising would not have led to a satisfactory control of the actual crack defects. Both the frequency and, particularly, the extent of present practice justify this statement. To some degree - especially con cerning surveys durings building - this must be attributed to the lack of attention which was paid to the actual detail of design during planning as well by designers as by those involved in the classification society. The Commission will, however, add that it is our opinion that there is no reason to believe that conditions would have been essentially diffe rent if another classification society had been responsible for the control of «Alexander L. Kielland». In the report the Norwegian Maritime Direc torate is exposed to scrutiny for the way its superior responsibility for the safety control of the structure has been executed. The Maritime Directorate has, in accordance with the possibility opened up in the regulations, based its approval on valid certificate of the classification society only. This way of proce dure will not give the Maritime Directorate the opportunity to check if the classification society is administering its rules in accord ance with its content. It should in the actual case be pointed to the fact that the practice which has been followed, in particular by controlling the design is not in full compliance with the Maritime Directorate's own, relatively general regulations. Concerning the loss of stability the main reason for the unintended heeling, flooding and capsizing was that the regulations do not require that stability must be maintained after the loss of a complete column (a main buoyancy member). In addition deficient compliance with instructions regarding closing of closing appliances contributed to the consequences of the accident because the openings that became flooding openings in this way allowed rapid filling of water in the deck. This in turn reduced the time in which the rig remained floating in a position that could allow per sonell to be rescued by helicopter etc. without entering the sea. The chance of surviving more than half an hour in the cold water was, as it turned out to be, minimal. 6.4 LIFE-SAVING APPLIANCES ETC. AND THE RESCUE OPERATION «Alexander L. Kielland» was equipped with life-saving appliances as required by regula tions. Rescue training on board was generally speaking also carried out in accordance with regulations, however, to such a way that too much emphasis has been put on lifeboat practising. On the other hand it was only a few of the personell on board who in advance had been given the safety training which is required by regulations. As far as the crew and the catering personell on board is concerned, it is required that they have fulfilled an introduction course of three weeks duration

as the crew and the catering personell on board is concerned, it is required that they have fulfilled an introduction course of three weeks duration

side 210

pertaining to safety both theoretically and practically. Only four of the persons on board had attended such a course. In addition, one of the crew members had participated in short safety course of two weeks duration. Concer ning the so-called hotel quests it is not laid down in regulations precise requirments regarding safety training. «Arbeidsmil jøloven», which applies to employees (except for the signed personell) on floating platforms and fixed installations, however, implies that the employees shall be informed about the risks regarding accidents and health hazards which in this connection is necessary. Only a small number of the hotel quests had fulfilled any course and, moreover, these courses lasted essentially for one day. Of the 212 men on board «Alexander L. Kielland», on the day of the accident only 76 persons had attended any course. Among these 50 persons had just been through a course of one day's duration. As judged from the percentage of course participants among those who perished and those who survived, it appears that the type of training which has tåken place up to now does not itself improve the chance of surviving in a catastrophic situation. Other factors such as for example experience at sea, seems in this respect to be greater importance. With the list which «Alexander L. Kielland» almost immediately got after the loss of the column D, most people felt that there was no time to go to the cabin to catch the life jacket. As the boxes with life jackets at the rescue stations were soon emptied, there were quite a few which could not obtain life jackets. Of the 89 persons who were rescued 53 wore life jackets. Survival suit was not prescribed as life saving appliance. Only the crew and some of the hotel guests possessed such equipment. 8 persons managed to put on survival suits. 4 of them were rescued. 4 of the perished and 3 of the survivors had not managed to close the zips on their suits properly. On board «Alexander L. Kielland» there were 7 covered lifeboats, each with seats for 50 men. 4 of the lifeboats were lowered without particular problems. On the other hand, problems occurred with the release of the lifeboat hooks. The hooks which were equip ped with simultaneous release can not be released as long as they are under load and this was difficult to avoid due to the rough sea on the day of the accident. For this reason 3 of the boats were blown against the platform and crashed. On the fourth boat the afterpart of the wheelhouse was crashed. Through the opening caused by the crash a man managed to release the after hook by hand. Before that someone had succeeded in one way or another to release the forward hook. A fifth boat came down on the water bottom-up when the platform capsized. The hooks had been released in some way or another. People in the boat and people ouside it, managed by com mon efforts to turn it on even keel. With regard to the two lifeboats which were utilized there were 26 men on board the first one. A few hours after the accident these people were tåken up by a rescue helicopter. In the other boat there were 14 persons when it came down to the water. In addition, 19 persons were tåken up in the boat from the sea. Of the persons on board 12 men were later on tåken on board a supply boat while the other 21 persons were rescued by helicopters. Those who were on board the lifeboats claimed that they felt heavily chilly. On board «Alexander L. Kielland» there were inflatable rubber rafts both for lowering by crane and for throwing over board with a capacity for 400 men all together. Attempts were made to release some rafts. This was not successfull. Some rafts were, however, released directly due to the list of the plat form. On some of them the inflatable system was activated when the rafts hit the water. 3 men managed to board these rafts. These men were later on rescued by supply vessels. Inflatable rafts were also thrown over board from «Edda 2/7 C » . All together 9 men managed to board one of these rafts. They were all hoisted up by a British rescue helicopter. From another raft from «Edda 2/7 C» 4 men were rescued by a supply vessel. Of all those who must have ended up in the sea when the «Alexander L. Kielland» cap sized and who were not tåken on board lifeboats or managed to board rafts, only 14 persons were rescued. Of these 7 persons managed to swim up to «Edda 2/7 C» and were tåken on board by means of personel basket. The other 7 were tåken up by supply vessels either directly from the sea or from wreckage which they clinged to. Most of those who were tåken up from the sea, were rescued within half an hour at a maximum. One man which had put on both survival suit and a life jacket was lying in the sea, however, for 2 hours and 35 minutes. In accordance with the approved emergency plan for the Ekofisk field 3 stand by vessels are to be placed in the field. Each stand-by vessel shall be stationed in such a position that it can reach each of the plat forms which it is intended to serve within the course of 20-25 minutes. The stand-by vessel for «Alexander L. Kielland» did not arrive at the place of the accident before nearly an hour

within the course of 20-25 minutes. The stand-by vessel for «Alexander L. Kielland» did not arrive at the place of the accident before nearly an hour

side 211

after the loss. No survivors were rescued by the stand-by vessel. A few minutes after the accident the Rescue Coordination Center, Stavanger, was alerted. The Rescue Coordination Center, among others, applied for assistance from rescue centers abroad and took care of directing vessels, aircrafts and helicopters to the area. The search and rescue operation all together involved 8 helicopters from Helikopter Ser vice A/S and 1 1 Sea King rescue helicopters. 3 of the rescue helicopters were Norwegian. 4 British, 2 West-German and 2 Danish. In addition 6 British Nimrod aircrafts and one Norwegian Orion aircraft participated in the operation. All together 71 ei vil vessels and 9 navy vessels were engaged in the search and rescue operation. 3 of the navy vessels came from Norway 3 from Great Britain, 2 from Netherlands and 1 from Denmark. As the activity increased it became difficult to main tain the general view and thereby to co ordinate the airforces and the vessels in the area. The Rescue Coordination Center accor dingly asked the British Nimrod aircraft to take the command of the air traffic and a Dutch destroyer to act in a similar function concerning ships in the area. Both the Nimrod aircraft and the destroyer performed their tasks eminently. About an hour after the accident the Cen tral Hospital in Rogaland established its catastrophic office. Three quarter of an hour later a team from this office went to Sola airport where a reception center for injured people was established. In addition a volunt ary team were flown out to «Ekofisk Hotel» where the team participated in reception and registration of rescued and perished people. The rescued people were given first aid when necessary. All together there were 212 men on board «Alexander L. Kielland» when the accident happened. 123 men lost their lives. 6.5 THE COMMISSION's EVALUATIONS AND RECOMMENDATIONS The safety level for floating platforms should and can be improved. The Commis sion would strongly emphasize that planning of actions aiming at improving the safety level, should be undertaken on the basis of a general approach. This implies that the total risk spectrum, depending upon the actual type, function and operational mode of the platform, must be assessed by a risk analysis approach. Hazards which may initiate structural or mooring line failure, loss of floating ability and stability, loss of electric power etc. and the interaction between such hazards, which subsequently lead to loss of human lives and/ or monetary values, must be considered. Also, hazards which directly may cause injury to personnel must be included. Furthermore, risk factors relating to hard ware - such as the structure and equipment, fabrication and operational procedures and personell must be considered. In particular risks relating to human actions or missions must be recognized. A unified approach is necessary to obtain harmonized safety requirements within the risk tolerance level acceptable to the society. Unified approach as recommended by the Commission has up to now be practiced to an adequate degree for mobile platforms. This is demonstrated by some examples in the report. In this summary it should be men tioned for example that while requirements regarding buoyancy and stability in damaged condition have been in existance for some time, there have been no requirements, however, for built in strength for such cases. The need for an overall approach is also supported by the fact that various safety requirements determining the layout - for instance of the platform deck - may be in conflict with each other. Thus, requirements of load carrying capacity, fire resisting bulk heads, and watertight bulkheads to obtain additional buoyancy, imply few and small openings. On the other hand access in particular in connection with evacuation, will be improved by håving as large and many openings as possible. An overall risk analysis approach is parti cularly important when new platforms with increased size, new layout, function etc. is planned. In the years to come new platform technology is expected to emerge. A complete safety evaluation should be accomplished in the planning phase. Then the largest potential for implementing effi cient risk reduetion actions - by reducing the probability of hazards and their consequ ences. At the same time, the approval of the planning of new platforms should preferably take place at two stages, namely the concept and detailed design stage. In this way possible changes of the overall layout found necessary during the control, can be most easily accomodated. On the other hand errors and omissions occurring in the planning phase may be difficult to identify and eliminate later on, and thus lead to hazards during building and in particular operation. It is, therefore, of the utmost importance that planning take place in organisations with dedicated management

building and in particular operation. It is, therefore, of the utmost importance that planning take place in organisations with dedicated management

side 212

of the safety and that the work is carried out by highly qualified people. In this connection the Commission points to the general need for extending the education also to include systematic safety education based on practi cal experience as well as on results from research and development efforts. This applies to university- as well as to vocational educational level. It is particularly important to utilize experiences from previous accidents based on analysis of all relevant factors which have contributed to the accident. It is also necessary to arrive at better systems for exchange of information regarding accidents. The information must be presented in suffici ent detail. The Commission is of the opinion that governmental authorities should have the overall responsibility for the safety of mobile units. The responsibility should include con trol of the planning, fabrication and operation of the platform, pertaining to the strength of the hull and anchoring system, floating abil ity and stability, fire resistance, evacuation and life-saving system etc. - and not at least the interaction between all these factors. The governmental efforts should be limited to drawing up main lines in the safety control work. In the opinion of the Commission the detailed control should to a larger extent than up to now be delegated to other institutions as for example the classification societies. A satisfactory safety control måkes it necessary to involve a large number of specialists with a high level of competency. The availability of qualified personnel is, however, limited and will also be limited in the years to come. For this reason and because the classification societies already have established an organi zation in order to cover both planning, con struction and also to a certain degree - and an increasing degree - operation, only a modera te increase of the governmental apparatus for safety control of mobile platforms seems to be necessary. The safety control of mobile platforms exercised by Norwegian authorities has been based somewhat uncritically on experience from ships design and operation without fully recognizing the particulars of mobile units. Now, there are greater differences between various platform concepts and between plat forms and ships than between various ship types. Continued development of platform technology is likely to bring about new con cepts. The Commission is, therefore, of the opinion that the safety management of mobile units should be better organized, to acknowledge the particulars of platforms and ensure an adequate control. This may be achieved by assigning particular responsibili ties to certain persons in his respect, prefer ably organized in a separate department or group, besides doing safety control work, this personnel should - to a larger extent than up to-now - follow-up research and development work relating to platform systems. By considering the general items outlined above as well as the particular recommenda tions with regard to strength, stability and life-saving appliances a reevaluation of the safety management of mobile units should be accomplished. In particular rules and regula tions should be established and followed-up in order to ensure an improved safety level which is consistent among all types of plat forms. The Commission's recommendations regarding design for structural integrity will be summed up in brief in the following. Per definition the construction fails when the load it is exposed to exceed its capacity. Failure can occur fundamentally for two reasons. Firstly, it can take place because of a built-in probability that the load effect will exceed the capacity of the structure. For instance, platforms are being dimensioned to day for a chosen finite level of wave loading. In this way there will still be a certain probability that the load will exceed the chosen value and accordingly a breakdown might be the result. The other main reason for failure is an abnormal load or abnormal capacity due to structural defects. Such abnormal conditions can most often be traced back to unqualified actions, errors or omissions at the planning building, or operation stage. In a few cases, however, the reason for the abnormal conditi ons may be truly unkkown phenomena which are not can not be accounted for. It follows that various - short and long term strategies should be used to achieve the desired safety level for mobile rig operations. The Commission has pointed to the following methods which more or less already have been tåken into use: Design and dimension the structure and the anchoring system to have an adequate safety against failure and so that direct consequences of the failure are limited. Plan the layout of the structure and equip ment and the accomplishment of building and operation so as to minimize the prob ability of errors during construction and particularly operation and so that the con trol (for instance inspection) can be carried out as simple as possible. Plan the control or supervising system for the planning, building and operation stage in order to identify and eliminate faults.

simple as possible. Plan the control or supervising system for the planning, building and operation stage in order to identify and eliminate faults.

side 213

Develop, transfer and utilize new knowledge and skill. In order to obtain improved safety for mobile platforms it is, in the opinion of the Commission, necessary to incorporate all these planning - and control strategies in the regulations and to follow them up in practice. The first of the mentioned strategies is the most direct and easy to implement in prac tice. The fact that no control system or other efforts can completely eliminate errors and omissions that may cause abnormal loads during operation and/or structural defects during fabrication and operation should be recognized. The Commission, therefore, recommends to consider small abnormal loads and reduction of built-in strength due to structural defects that occur relatively fre quently, in the dimensioning of the structure. At the same time it is important to ensure that a small structural damage can not progress and thereby result in extensive consequences - such as collapse of the whole structure or capsizing. Principles for design taking abnor mal conditions into account should be worked out. Special attention should be paid to establishing criteria for the damage and the associated loading for dimensioning check of damaged structures. These criteria should be compatible with those used in the evaluation of damage stability. Further guidance should be given regar ding the design of structural details including welded connections and in particular fillet weld connections. Rules and regulations should contain a basis for rational design and dimensioning of fillet welds utilized in diffe rent connections. The Commission recommends that mobile platforms should be planned as far as possible and to a larger extent than up-to-now so as to adapt the structure for surveys, and possibly, inthefuture,formonitoring.ltisalsonecessary to pay more attention to establishing realistic operation procedures, relating to the structu ral integrity. The control of the structural safety should include the planning-, construction- and opera tion-phase. The control system should primarily aim at discovering gross errors or omissions. Despite this limitation the control can not be complete. An efficient control therefore would depend upon a systematization through the use of check lists etc. for planning control, by the application of inspection manuals for the construction and operation phases etc. The inspection manual should include information about priority of places to inspect, inspection procedures and methods to be used, as well as criteria for acceptable damage or defects. The likelihood of damage and the possible consequences of damage should be the basis for establishing such a code of practice. However, a continuous up dating of the during fabrication and operation of the actual platform and for similar types of platforms. It is presupposed that a reporting system which is suitable for the purpose is established for the stages in question. The basis for the control of the planning of the platform system should be the concept itself and all necessary documentation such as calculations, drawings, material specifica tions, building- and operation procedures as well as the inspection handbook. A total risk analysis or thorough considerations should form the basis for evaluating the integrity of the structure, particularly when new types of platforms are concerned. As far as possible one should aim at independence in the con trol. The control institutions ought therefore to carry out independent calculations. Even if a platform has been subject to approval once it is of great importance to carry out a new real control if this type of platform is going to be built a few years later. New knowledge arrived at in the meantime might change the way in which the platform design is appraised. Because the purpose of the inspections during construction and operation should be to discover grave defects, the inspections should aim at covering most critical areas rather than extensively covering a small inspection instructions has to take place when defects etc. are experienced area in order to find minor damages, as sometimes has been the case up to now. In order that latent crack formation caused by the building process shall be released, it will be of great importance to carry out an exten sive control of a new platform on the light waterline in sheltered water after the first complete winter when it has been into con tinuous operation. Otherwise a main survey carried out with two years interval should be sufficient. This recommendation is given by the Commission on the condition that the hull inspection is carried out with the consider able better coverage and solidity than have been the case up to now. Further, more attention should be paid to better system for education, training and certification of inspectors. The control exer cised by authorities during construction and operation is necessarily periodic. During the time between these inspections the control has to be performed by the crew on board. The content of this internal control ought to be

During the time between these inspections the control has to be performed by the crew on board. The content of this internal control ought to be

side 214

more clearly spelled out, and followed-up by motivation, education etc. of the operation personell. As regards the Commissions recommenda tions regarding the platforms ability to float in a stable position, some of the most impor tant items are mentioned below. Loss of stability and its consequences should in general be counteracted by designing the platform to withstand capsizing and flooding with adequate safety margins, and by implementing strategies to avoid errors and omissions in planning and operation and a control system to detect and eliminate possi ble faults. Specifically, this implies that damage stability criteria ought to be reevaluated. Thus, the location and extent of the initial damage or degree of waterfilling that initiate a damaged/flooded condition should be established on the basis of the experienced gained in the «Alexander L. Kielland» case and analyses of other hazardous situations. Among other things, mobile platforms have lost a major buoyancy member on other occasions. Stability regulations should also be estab lished as to ensure that there is a realistic safety margin with respect to capsizing and progressive flooding when taking into account all uncertainties that pertain to formal stability control at the planning stage. Among other things, the effect of waves and mooring system is not tåken directly into account in the stability control. Wind moment calculations are especially uncer tain. The analysis model used to calculate flooding is simplified. A reevaluation of damage stability criteria would lead to requirements for reserve buoyancy. For some platforms such a require ment should be complied with by requiring reserve buoyancy in the deck structure. The requirement ought, however, to be formu lated independent of the type of structure, because the additional buoyancy may be provided in other ways in some platforms. The requirement should be functional and not specific. Only in this way the design of the deck can be rational, taking all, possible, conflicting, safety requirements into account. At the planning stage significant efforts can be made to reduce the occurence of operatio nal errors - particularly in emergency situa tions. This can, for instance, be achieved through the design of the platform by appropriate location and layout of the marine control room, and location of openings and selection of closing appliances and method of closing for them. Furthermore, more emphasis ought to be placed on realistic operation-procedures and the crew's know ledge, understanding and application of such. Motivation, education and training - by the use of simulators for maneuvring, ballasting, mooring operations etc. form the basis for the risk reduction achieved in this way. The design of the platform should as far as possible not be based on assumptions regarding execution of emergency operations in connec tion with closing appliances, ballast opera tions etc. The fundamental principles for controlling the stability, should be laid down in regula tions. Besides, more specific instructions regarding the practical application of the general rules would be useful. Detailed, non mandatory guidelines for stability calcula tions, interpretation of damage stability criteria, location of openings and technical standard of closing appliances to prevent the openings from being down-flooding openings, together with operation procedures should be included as appendices to the rules. The control of stability at the planning stage should be based on an overall view of the platform system. Independent control of stability calcula tions - by means of computerized methods with adequate accuracy - should be strived for. The fulfillment of stability requirements would normally be sensitive to the location of potential down-flooding openings. Therefore, particular attention should be paid to the control of such an item. The checking especially of procedures and limitations for emergency operations pertain ing to stability - as established in the operat ing manual - ought to be tightened up. Despite the fact that abnormal weight conditions do not seem to be a contributing factor to the actual accident other informa tion obtained by the Commission, indicate that an improved system for weight control during operation should be established. This system would in volve an inclining test for any new rig and a continuous registering and reporting of weight, with the possibility of spot checks by deplasement and inclining tests during operation. Consideration should also be made of deve loping an improved method for checking that operation instructions - especially for emer gency situations - are known, understood and can be applied by the maritime personnel. Concerning contingency plans, life-saving appliances etc. the Commission's recom mendations are aiming at improving the state of preparedness both on board the platforms

plans, life-saving appliances etc. the Commission's recom mendations are aiming at improving the state of preparedness both on board the platforms

side 215

themselves as well as the contingency plan ning outside the platforms. Some of the recommendations regarding contingency planning at large will first be outlined. The Commission proposes, possibly through requirements laid down by the authorities, a co-operation between the oper ators in an area in order to ascertain the necessary standard. By a co-ordination of the contingency planning of various operators a more rapid and more effective assistance is expected from other operators who are not directly concerned in an emergency situation. With regard to stand-by vessels the Com mission is proposing that the same arrange ment should be required for all types of platforms: drilling rigs, hotel platforms and fixed installations and that the requirements should be in accordance with what has been required for drilling rigs up to now. This implies that for each platform one stand-by vessel shall be permanently stationed at a distance of not more than one nautical mile from the platform. The Commission further proposes that the supply vessels should be equipped with life saving appliances which put them in the position to pick up people from the sea in an efficient way. It is further proposed to di vide the continen tal shelf into helicopter rescue zones. A rescue helicopter with permanently mounted rescue hoist and with a crew educated and trained for rescue operations should be located in each zone. Helicopters which are landbased and are transporting personell to and from the plat forms, might be utilized in a case of emergency in a more efficient way than has been the case up to now. The Commission recommends that the possibility of doing so is explored. The Commission proposes that at least once a year a co-ordinated exercise should take place by activating all rescue resources on the field included in the contingency plan, eventually the coordinated contingency plan for more operators. Also the operators' con tingency plans on shore should participate including management and co-ordination of the operation, testing of communication lines and so on. The Commission's recommendations regarding life-saving appliances etc. on the platform itself partly concern extension of capacity of a few of the existing life-saving appliances, partly improing the standard of existing life-saving appliances and partly new appliances. Firstly, the Commission recom mends a lifeboat coverage of 200 per cent for all types of platforms. The proposal is in accordance with the actual requirement for fixed installations today. The need for an overcapacity of lifeboats does not seem to be less for drilling rigs and hotel platforms. For the latter types of platforms it seems realistic to take into account the fact that about half the number of lifeboats may not be operative due to damage or be unavailable because of fire, heeling, unfavourable weather condi tions or a combination of these factors. The Commission further proposes that each plat form shall have a coverage of inflatable rafts of 200 per cent up to 100 persons and 100 per cent coverage for the number of persons exceeding 100. For drilling rigs this proposal will in practice not lead to any change in the inflatable raft coverage. For the two other types of platforms the capacity of inflatable rafts will have to be increased somewhat. Taking into account the increased coverage of lifeboats which the Commission has prop osed, a launching system for parts of the inflatable rafts is considered to be unneces sary. The recommendations given for improve ment of existing life-saving appliances is only concerned with lifeboats. In the actual acci dent it was the releasing mechanism of the lifeboats which failed. This releasing system will not necessarily be improved by the introduction of lifeboat hooks which can be released while under load. Experience from a previous accident shows that the possibility then exists that release due to panic can take place at high level above the sea. The Com mission, therefore, recommends to intensify the work to improve releasing mechanism or to develop another launching arrangement. In the meantime the Commission proposes certain alterations of the relasing mechanism as for example strengthening of the cover plates fitted around the releasing hook in order to avoid that the plates are being bent and thereby might block up the releasing hook. Further the Commission proposes a number of improvements regarding equip ment and supplies such as clothing and woollen carpets stored on board lifeboats, reflexes to be fitted on the outside of the superstructure and the bottom of the boats etc. In addition to existing life-saving appliances the Commission proposes that personell basket and survival suits are required as life-saving appliances. Concer ning survival suits the Commission proposes that each man on board shall have his personal survival suit placed in the cabin. In addition the Commission proposes an additional coverage of survival suits of 200 per

shall have his personal survival suit placed in the cabin. In addition the Commission proposes an additional coverage of survival suits of 200 per

side 216

cent. These suits should be adequately placed where evacuation takes place and at the lifeboat stations. The Commission is of the opinion that the experiences from this acci dent imply that such a high coverage of survival suits is necessary to ensure that everybody who is on board a platform shall have a reasonable chance to get hold of a survival suit in a situation which requires rapid evacuation. In larger dayrooms and corridors the inven tory and larger stores should be fixed. The layer on the floor must give sufficient friction to avoid sliding. Corridors and evacuation ways should be equipped with separate lamps coupled to batteries which automatically engage in the case that main power sources as well as emergency power sources fail. Regarding qualification requirements for those who work or live on board a platform the Commission is of the opinion that there is no reason why only the maritime crew and catering personell shall attend an introduc tion safety course. Other employees and the so-called hotel guests have at least the same need for safety training as the crew. Dispensa tion from the safety training course should only be given in exceptional cases. The safety training and the exercises on board shall include training concerning all life-saving appliances with which a platform is equipped. The training on board should be extended also to include testing of the evacuation ways. When a person has arrived on board a platform for the first time he should immediately be shown around. During this guided tour he should be explicitly explained where the different parts of the life-saving equipment is placed. He should also be shown the lifeboat station to which he belongs and the evacuation ways which lead to this sta tion. The education and training should in particular aim at developing the safety con sciency of the participants. The value of education and training will be very much reduced for participants who are not moti vated for safety education and training. The Commission recommends that clear guidelines for the instruction to be given a new man coming on board a platform before he is commencing his duty. These guidelines should be approved by governmental author ities. The Commission's considerations and recommendations are unanimous regarding all points.

side 217

VEDLEGG Forord Vedleggene inneholder utvalgt teknisk do kumentasjon vedrørende plattformens plan legging, bygging og operasjon samt av de undersøkelser som er foretatt etter ulykken. Det er ikke mulig å gjengi de omfattende utredninger som er gjort av sakkyndige for kommisjonen, fullstendig innenfor en rapport med rimelig omfang. Kommisjonen har der for, med ett unntak, foretatt et utdrag av disse tekniske rapportene, som sammen er referert i vedleggene. Således er siste underavsnitt av vedlegg 3-10 benyttet som referanseliste både for sentral teknisk dokumentasjon - enten den er referert til i vedleggene eller benyttet bare som grunnlag for hoveddelen av rap porten.

Vedlegg 1 1.3.5 Scuppers b) The following weathertight doors are pre ferably to be kept closed in bad weather: The scuppers including: - 3 in Z bay - 10 in A bay - 5 in B bay - 10 in C bay - 1 in D bay - 1 in F bay doors of companion-way of B bay (2 doors) doors of companion-way of F bay (2 doors) 1.3.3 Hatches The hatches include: 2 hatches on Z bay (1 main - 1 emergency) 2 hatches on D bay shall be closed in storm conditions and in case of damage. 2 hatches on G bay 1.3.6 Man-holes These hatches are preferably to be kept closed in bad weather. The following man-holes must be perma nently closed at sea: 1.3.4 Ventilation openings - Man-holes for access in pontoon dry-bilges - Man-holes for access in pontoon ballasts - Man-holes between pontoon ballasts - Man-holes for access in pile ballasts - Man-holes for access thruster tunnels - Man-holes for access thruster capsules - Man-holes for access oblique bracings - Man-holes for access upper joints 1.3.4.1 Ventilation openings on pile heads (10 openings ) The air inlets and outlets of each pile companion must be kept permanently clo sed at sea in storm conditions. They must also be kept permanently closed at draught over 18 m when there is a risk of damage to the piles (approach of supply boats, icebergs, floatsams etc. . .) - Man-holes for access upper horizontal bra cings. They are allowed to be open at draughts over 18 m under the condition that a man should stay in the vicinity, ready to close them in case of emergency. 3. Transit conditions 3.1 Draught The normal draught for towing is 7,5 m but the platform is to be submerged at a minimum 16 m draught when either one of the following conditions occurs: 1.3.4.2 Ventilation openings in P 5 beam They shall be kept ciosed in storm conditi ons or after damage. - the angular motion (roll and pitch) is over the requirements of the sheet 53 (komm. anm.: vist i figur nedenfor). 1.3.4.3 Ventilation openings above the main deck - when V 3 to x h of the length of any lower horizontal bracing is submerged by waves. They are permanently open. Allowable roll angles and period for main structure in towing conditions Full cycle period, seconds

side 221

Vedlegg 1 5. Emergency conditions 5.1 Storm conditions 5.1.1 Draught In case of storm such that the air gap between the lower deck and the maximum wave crest elevation is lower than 1,50 m, the draught must be reduced to 18 m. Detailed instructions for the required de ballasting must be available on board with indication of the tonnages of salt water to be transferred. These tonnages must be perma nently up-to-dated in terms of the true loa ding and the true LCG and VCG. Vedlegg 1 «Alexander L. Kielland»-ulykken Forhalingsprosedyre for «Alexander L. Kiel land» som hotellplattform ved fast installa sjon. (Ikke spesifisert i driftshåndboken) ningsutstyret endret eller komplettert. Det utstyret plattformen hadde før forliset skal sammenfattes i dette vedlegg. Livbåter Ved den siste oppankringen ved «Edda 2/7 C» var avstanden mellom riggen og fast installasjon 18 til 30 m. Av sikkerhetsgrunner måtte riggen trekkes ut fra den faste plattfor men i hardt vær. Eksisterende regelverk (Sjøfartsdirektoratet, Veritas) omtaler ikke kriterier for forhaling til «stand-off»-posisjon. Båt nr. 1-2-3-4-5 av typen 26' «Harding Safety MC tank vessel, overseas voyage», med kapasitet for 50 personer. Båt nr. 6-7 av typen 26' «Harding Safety MCF tank vessel, overseas voyage», med kapasitet for 50 personer. Livbåt nr. 5 var betjent av en «David Company davit type FDA 4000». For de andre 6 livbåtene var benyttet davit fra Nordavit (Mjølner Industrier). Driftshåndboken for «Alexander L. Kiel land» inneholder heller ikke slik veiledning. Imidlertid er dette berørt i forbindelse med lokasjonsgodkjennelsene. I den siste loka sjonsgodkjennelsen, datert 29.11.1979, heter det at forhaling må foretas når strekket i ankerline overskrider 0,78 MN (80 t) eller når plattformsjefen anser det nødvendig på grunn av dønninger, strøm, vind eller andre årsaker. Således har noen plattformsjefer tatt gangbroen og forhalt når vindhastigheten har nådd opp i 20-25 m/s. Samtlige livbåter hang i kroker av «Mills» type, som kan utløses i avlastet tilstand. Dette var eneste godkjente prinsipp for liv båtkroker. Samtlige båter var plasser på nedre dekks nivå. Livbåt nr. 1: Akterut på babord side under helikopterdekk. Praksis når det gjelder forhaling har nok variert noe. Livbåt nr. 2: Akterut på styrbord side under helikopterdekk. Før forhalingen pleide gangbroen å bli skjøvet inn i en port, og sperring satt opp. Selve forhalingen skjedde ved slakking og str amming av ankerlifene. Ankervinsjene ble operert på hver søyle ved større forhalinger. Ved mindre forhalinger ble de operert sentralt fra kontrollrommet. Hvis ikke plattformsje fen var der, ledet kontrollromsoperatøren vinsj eoperasjonene. Via «walki-talki» sto le delsen i kontrollrommet i kontakt med en person på dekk som overvåket forhalingen derfra. Ankervinsjene ble betjent av ulike personer. Dette ble for eksempel på siste oppankringssted ordnet slik at en operatør kjørte linene på B og D-søylene, mens en annen hadde ansvaret for linene på A- og E søylene. Med forhaling ut fra den faste instal lasjonen ble linene på B- og D-søylene slakket mens de andre ble strammet. Strekket i linene ble overvåket under hele operasjonen. Hvis strekket i linene overskred 0,6 til 0,7 MN, ble propellene benyttet for å holde riggen roligere og jevne ut strekket i ankerlinene. Livbåt nr. 3: Forut på babord side. Livbåt nr. 4: Forut på styrbord side. Livbåt nr. 5: På utsiden av søyle B. Livbåt nr. 6: På forsiden (ca. kl. 11) av søyle D. Livbåt nr. 7: På forsiden (ca. kl. 13) av søyle B. Oppblåsbare flåter 12 stk. gummiflåter av typen «Beaufort 20Q», lårbare med kapasitet 20 personer hver. På ulykkesdagen var 4 stk. 20Q lårbare flåter i land for årlig inspeksjon. Ifølge vitne utsagn fantes det ombord 6-8 stk. reserveflå ter som ble benyttet når flåter var inne for inspeksjon. De oppblåsbare flåtene var plassert slik: Lårbare 20Q: 3 stk. på søyle A. 3 stk. på søyle E. 3 stk. mellom søylene B og C. 3 stk. mellom søylene C og D. Linestrekket ble til slutt justert slik at det ble passe strekk i alle liner. Deretter ble bremsene satt på fra kontrollrommet. Kast-over-bord 20X: 6 stk. akterut under helikopterdekk. 4 stk. mellom søylene B og C. 2 stk. mellom søylene C og D. Ved kjøring av vinsjene begrenses kreftene i ankersystemet av en maksimal trekk-kraft på 1,23 MN og en bremsekraft på 2,94 MN. Mann-over-bord båt 1.4 OMFANG OG PLASSERING AV LIV REDNINGSUTSTYR OMBORD 1 stk. av typen Harding 20M/«pickup-boat» for tankskip, «overseas voyage - N.T.», kapa sitet 20 personer. Generelt Etter som «Alexander L. Kielland» ble ombygd for en økt hotellkapasitet ble livredDavit: Nordavit type OPD-312. Plassering på nedre dekk mellom søyle B og C.

«Alexander L. Kielland» ble ombygd for en økt hotellkapasitet ble livredDavit: Nordavit type OPD-312. Plassering på nedre dekk mellom søyle B og C.

Vedlegg 2 lianican 18) •nm

side 232

; an<err 13) Rappon til Sji> feroe iroxtcretet '■>•• Ark nr. 2. Verneombud var med under besiktelsen. En hadde også møte med hovedverneumbud fra Brownaker. Han nevnte under møte at de ombord Eldfisk Bravo plattformen var fra 221 til 230 personer ombord mens tillatelsen kun gjalt for 200 personer. Han skulle ta dette opp med ledelsen da han fikk bekreftet at dette var ulovlig. Likeledes nevnte han at en livbåt på Tor 7 Alphaplattformen ville treffe fenderne under låring Dette bor sees nærmere på under besøk på nevnte plattform. Båt- og brannøvelse ble avholdt under tilsyn av teknisk og sjøkjndig skips inspektør. Plattformens faste mannskap kjente til sine plasser og gjøremål, men der var problem for de innkvarterte personer å finne sine plasser. Den eneste opplysning de hadde om sin båtplass var oppslag på innsiden av lugardør. Båtførerne hadde heller ikke noe sjekkliste med navnt eller nummer, de måtte derfor kjenne personlig de personer som skulle være i båtene. Dette ble ikke ansett tilfredsstillende da en vil påstå at det er umulig for en person å ha oversikt over 50 personer i en nødsituasjon uten muligheter fe å sjekke navn etc. Det ble anbefalt å utarbeide et lignende system som brukes på Frigg-feltet, hvor hver person får utlevert kort med båt stasjons nr. etc. og samtlige båtførere har listen som er ajourført over hvilken personer som skal møte opp ved deres båtstasjon. Da vinden var opp til 40 knops styrke med stor sjø, ble ikke båtene satt på vannet. Båtene ble derimot låret 3-4 meter og endebryter ble prøvet, dette virket tilfredsstillende. «Alexander L. Kielland»-ulykken

side 247

Vedlegg 3 3.3 KONTROLL AV INNHOLDET I TAN KENE I SØYLE D ETTER FORLISET Ifølge vedlegg 3.2 var innholdet i tankene på søyle D på «Alexander L. Kielland» som vist i tabell 3.3.1. Tabe113.3.1 Innholdpåtankeneisøyle D på « Alexander L. Kielland» den 27 .03 .80, se vedlegg 3.2 Total ballastmengde: 1260 tonn. Deplasement og tyngdepunktsbeliggenhet for søyle D ble beregnet basert på oppmåling av dennes posisjon i sjøen. Deplastementet ble funnet å være 2670 tonn. Stålvekten av søylen, inkludert utstyr og deler av avbrukne stag, ble beregnet til 867 tonn, se ref. /3.1/. Ifølge ref. /3.1/ befinner lufterørene for bal lasttankene 8, 9 og 10 seg under vann og er åpne. Disse tankene vil derfor være delvis fylt. Videre har trunken stått åpen slik at det har skjedd delvis vannfylling. Antas tankene 8, 9 og 10; og trunken å være fylt opp til vannlinjen utenfor, vil disse inne holde henholdsvis 511 og 231 tonn. Det kan konstateres at tilstanden for trunken var slik som antydet. Følgende oppstilling kan da gjøres for avviket mellom angitt ballast og virkelig ballast: Tabell 3.3.2 Mulig avvik mellom oppgitt og virkelig ballast i søyle D. Denne sammenligningen viser at D-søylens tilstand ved ulykken ikke kan avvike mer enn 200-300 tonn fra den oppgitte. Da man må anta at det har kommet en del vann inn i tankene 8, 9 og 10, vil avviket være mindre enn den maksimale verdi som fremkom av oven stående. 3.4 KONTROLL AV BALLAST OG ANNEN VÆSKELAST I KONSTRUKSJONEN FORØVRIG ETTER FORLISET For å sikre mer reserveoppdrift av plattfor men mens den etter havariet lå med ponton gene i vannflaten utenfor Kårstø, ble det pumpet luft inn i trunken på søylene C og E, samt flere tanker på søyle E og noen få tanker på søylene A, B og C. Ut fra loggen fra disse operasjonene fremgår det at trunken på søyle E måtte være vannfylt. Dypgangen for plattformen ble registrert på de fire pontongene før og etter at luft ble pumpet inn i trunkene. Dette gir et grunnlag for å estimere lettskipsvekt og tyngdepunk tets beliggenhet for den havarerte plattform. Sammen med informasjon om vektsforhol dene for søyle D, avbrutte stag, boretårn, helikopterdekk, samt diverse løst utstyr som ble revet av eller falt av plattformen og vann som kom inn i plattformen kunne det derved være mulig å kontrollere den intakte platt forms lettskipsvekt og tyngdepunktsbeligTank

og vann som kom inn i plattformen kunne det derved være mulig å kontrollere den intakte platt forms lettskipsvekt og tyngdepunktsbeligTank

side 248

«Alexander L. Kielland»-ulykken genhet. Denne kontrollen ble ikke utført da det hefter mange og store usikkerheter til dette. Den vesentligste bunner i vanskelighe ten med å fastlegge hvor og hvor mye vann som var kommet inn i plattformen på ethvert tidspunkt. Således kunne en konstatere at det kom luft ut og vann inn mens plattformen lå ved Kårstø. En eventuell vektskontroll burde derfor utestå til plattformen var snudd. Det er imidlertid ikke noe som tyder på vesentlige feil i de opplysninger som er gitt i vedlegg 3.2 om ballastmengde og -fordeling. Under arbeidet med å snu plattformen høsten 1980, viste det seg nødvendig å etable re indre overtrykk i en del skråstag for å unngå knekning av disse. Trykkmålingene viste at stagene ikke var vanntette. Trykket kunne ikke bringes over 3,2 til 3,4 bar, selv med omfattende pumping av luft, ref. /3.2/. Dette indikerer at skråstagene lekket ved nedre dekksnivå. Det fremgikk også under snuingsarbeidet at beliggenheten av plattformens tyngde punkt avvek fra det som ble antatt i utgang spunktet. Det var først da riggen var snudd til 120° vinkel at følsomheten med hensyn på tyngdepunktsbeliggenheten ble stor nok til at dette ble oppdaget, ref. /3.2/. Årsakene til avviket kan blant annet være feil i opprinneli ge vektsdata, feil i variable dekkslaster og i ballast på tankene.

side 249

«Alexander L. Kielland»-ulykken Kommentarer Inspeksjonen er fullstendig m.h.p. åpninger på søyletoppene, unntatt for søyle A. Inspek sjonen der ble forhindret på grunn av wirer, tau etc. Når en del av dørene er inntrykket, skyldes dette sannsynligvis primært ytre vann-over trykk etter at plattformen er kantret. Dørene er ikke dimensjonert for det opptredende vanntrykket. Dørene må nødvendigvis ha vært stengt for at inntrykningen skulle kunne skje. De dørene som er åpne, og uten skade må med overveiende sannsynlighet ha vært åpne i ulykkesøyeblikket, eller blitt åpnet under rømningsaksj onen. 3.6 REFERANSER 3.1 Ludvigsen, J.A.: «Beregning av ballast i skaft D», Notat for Kommisjonen, 17.04.80. 3.2 Walker, S., m.fl.: «Technical Report on Current Status of the «Alexander L. Kiel land» uprighting project», Joint Venture Consortium for the Uprighting and Pre servation of the «Alexander L. Kielland », datert 11.11.80.

side 251

Vedlegg 4. Havmiljødata for Ekofisk-området 4.1 HAVMILJØET DEN 27. MARS Datakilder Havmiljødata for ulykkesdagen er basert på to hovedkilder. Den første kilden er direkte instrumentelt målte vind-, strøm- og bølgeda ta på Phillips' plattform «2/4 H» på Ekofisk feltet og analysert av Vassdrags- og Havne laboratoriet i Trondheim. Bølgemålingene ble foretatt med en Plessey radar over 17 minutters perioder hver tredje time. Vindma lingene ble utført med et anemometer Skyva ne WlOl-P-HF-360 i en høyde av 87,6 m. Middelvindhastigheten over 9 min. ble regi strert. Strømmåleren var trolig av type Mcßirney 555 og var plassert omtrent 12 m under middelvannstanden. Den andre kilden er data fra værkart, vindobservsjoner og teoretisk prediksjon ba sert på meteorologiske data fra Værvarslinga på Vestlandet. Der er knyttet flere usikkerheter til regi strerte data. Den viktigste mangel er begrens ningen registreringene har med hensyn på varighet og registreringsområde. Også regi streringsteknikkene begrenser informasjo nen en får. F. eks. er det ikke mulig å registrere retningsfordelingen på bølgene med det ut styret som har vært brukt hittil. Observasjo nene, eller målingene, foretas hver 3. time. Variasjonen mellom registreringstidspunk tene får en da ingen informasjon om. Observa sjonsfeil, spesielt ved visuelle observasjoner, introduserer nye usikkerheter. Når en ønsker værdata for et bestemt område vil det bare unntaksvis være registre ringer fra det aktuelle området. Registrerin ger fra andre områder i nærheten vil da bli brukt. For ulykkesdagen er det utført bølge og vindmalinger ved «2/4H» -plattformen. Den ligger ca. 2 km nord for «Edda 2/7 C». Dybden på de to stedene er henholdsvis 70 og 80 m. Det er her usikkert om vindmalingene innsendt til Meteorologisk Institutt er normalisert til en høyde 10 m over middel vannstanden. I tillegg til ovennevnte usikkerheter, vil tilpasninger og antagelser knyttet til bruken av data (bestemmelse av for eksempel eks trem verdier) innføre nye usikkerheter. Direkte instrumentelt målte data vil nor malt være mest nøyaktige. Dessverre finnes det kun begrensede data fra målinger av bølgetilstanden. En må derfor ofte benytte bølgedata fra visuelle observasjoner eller teoretisk beregnede verdier basert på regi streringer av lufttrykk og vindhastigheter. Vinddata Instrumentelt målte vinddata fra «Ekofisk 2/4H» -plattformen er sammenlignet med and re data i fig. 4.1.1. Kilder for avvik er referanse til ulik geogra fisk posisjon for datainnsamling, observa sjonsfeil eller eventuelle målefeil, eller model leringsfeil og analysefeil i prediksjonsmeto dikk. Det fremgår av fig. 4.1.1 a hvordan mindre svingninger i middelvindhastigheten ikke kan fanges opp av observasjoner som skjer hver 3dje time. Den visuelt bestemte hastighet ligger syste matisk over de instrumentelle data. Dette kan skyldes at observatøren ikke har reudsert sine visuelle observasjoner fra 50 m's høyde til 10 m's høyde, som er referansehøyden for angivelse av vindhastighet.

observatøren ikke har reudsert sine visuelle observasjoner fra 50 m's høyde til 10 m's høyde, som er referansehøyden for angivelse av vindhastighet.
